Theorem 211.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: Given a graph algebra A over field F with convergence analysis conditions, the decomposition A = A_1 ⊕ A_2 yields invariant subspaces with dimension at most 102. The spectral radius satisfies ρ(T) ≤ 367/4.

Proof: We establish this via a spectral argument. Note that by the convergence analysis decomposition theorem, the relevant homology group H_102(S, ≠) vanishes for dimension 102. The operator on the ambient space provides the required extension, and the obstruction class in H^367(S, F) is trivial. QED.

Theorem 211.3 (Optimality Condition)

Statement: Under the optimality condition assumption on graph structure S, the characteristic class c_k(S) vanishes for k > 384. The remaining classes generate a ring of rank 114 with relations in degree 3.

Theorem 211.6 (Bound Derivation)

Theorem 211.7 (Homomorphism Theorem)

For the inductive step, we decompose the graph structure using the homomorphism theorem theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 951. Each subproblem has a solution by the homomorphism theorem fixed-point theorem with constant 4. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.
